The draft law was submitted to the National Assembly for consideration by 5,000 voters and voters. With regard to this proposal, the Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons Committee proposes to the National Assembly that it be decided that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration. ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u2	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Dear colleagues and respectable colleagues! Allow me to give you a brief presentation of the Committee's report. The Committee on Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons is at its 34th session of 11. On 1 this year, the draft law amending the Law on Marriage and Family Relations, submitted to the National Assembly by more than 5,000 electorates and electorates with a proposal for the consideration and adoption of this proposal for a short-term law, was considered as a mother’s working body. The College of the President of the National Assembly decided that the draft law amending the Law on Marriage and Family Relations should be dealt with in a reduced manner and that the Committee should also consider it under Rule 142 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly. Representatives of the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, representatives of the Ministry of Justice and the Legal and Legislative Service participated in the work of the Committee. The Committee was also given an opinion which examined the proposal in terms of its compliance with the Constitution, the legal system and the technical and legislative aspects and made specific comments. An amendment to Article 2 has been tabled by qualified investors within the time limit. The representative of the proponents presented the path of the proposed law requiring the collection of more than 5,000 citizens and citizens, voters and constituents of the Republic of Slovenia. Even so, as she pointed out, it seems unreasonable to her for a long time before this bill was discussed in the National Assembly. The representatives of the applicants also presented the objectives and content of the bill; this is in one word the protection of the child's benefits. The representative of the Legislative and Legal Service said that the draft law had been examined from the point of view of their competence. It also drew attention to the proposed provision, inter alia, to the removal of parental rights and stressed that parental rights are a constitutional right and a convention right. The withdrawal measure is therefore only allowed under exceptional circumstances. It also drew attention to the Institute of Relative Adoption, which is complex and therefore needs to be regulated in a comprehensive and weighty manner. It is also necessary to examine and amend the provisions of the parents' declaration on the choice of guardians established in legally comparable countries and to propose a better solution. In its opinion, the Government found that a comprehensive and not only partial, partial regulation of family law was needed, so the competent ministry, namely the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, drafted a proposal for a new family code. Those solutions require a comprehensive system of law, a coherent system of protection and education and protective measures for children in the field of care, care and adoption; and the delimitation of the real competence to decide on these matters. The partial solutions, as highlighted and resulting from the proposal for a law, are not legally completed. In the debate that followed, it was pointed out that the proposal for a law went through a purely normal parliamentary procedure and that we could not have dealt with it sooner under the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, since the procedure for a proposal for a law governing the same social relationship had to be completed before that. Proponents of the bill explicitly put forward the view that we have a legal void in the proposed area and that the amendment of the law must be adopted as soon as possible. Those who opposed and opposed this bill considered that the proposal was flawed and not ready to go into further procedure or procedure. There is a need for a comprehensive and not only partial, partial regulation of family law, so we do not support the bill. Some also considered that all essential rights under this proposal were already covered by the law in force. At the conclusion of the vote on the amendment tabled to Article 2, the Committee did not adopt the proposed amendment. Following the discussion, a proposal was made for the Committee to decide on a proposal for a decision that the proposal for a law was not suitable for further consideration. The proposal for a law amending the Law on Marriage and Family Relations is not suitable for further consideration, as nine Members have been identified for such a decision and only three have opposed it. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u5	The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. The word shall be given by the Group of the Democratic Party of the Pensioners of Slovenia, in its name mag. Juliana Bizjak Mlakar.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u6	Thank you very much for your words, Mr President.Dear Members, Members and other Members! The amendment to the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Marriage and Family Relations with a very similar content as we have before us today has recently been discussed at the 24th regular meeting of the National Assembly. As was then, in today's proposal for the amendment, the mother labour body concluded that the law was not suitable for further consideration. The bill provides for the removal of parental rights from a parent who has been detained for suspicion or has been convicted of offences of serious bodily harm, murder or murder of his spouse or partner, his child or the parents of his child. The proposal makes it possible for parents to adopt a child, with all the rights and duties applicable between parents and children in the family and in all other legal areas. According to the proposal for a new law, the Centre for Social Work also takes into account the wishes or suggestions of old parents when appointing a guardian. It can also take into account the last will of one or both dead parents, if that is in the child’s best interests. In the event of disagreement between the Centre for Social Work, the Ombudsman and the elderly parents and other close relatives, and the last will of the parents, the Court shall decide in an unfair proceeding. A child who does not have parents or who has been deprived of parental rights or whose parents do not care for him will be appointed a caregiver without delay. The arguments for rejecting the amendment are similar or almost identical to that of the amendment of the same law that we have recently considered in the National Assembly, which is understandable in view of the similar content of both laws. Even in this proposal, the amendment did not take into account all legal moments that may occur in cases where the grandson is no longer a grandchild; rather, he becomes his ex-parents when they become his parents. Not to mention or repeat everything that has been said recently, let me say that the proposed solution does not formally and comprehensively address all possible legal consequences that would have occurred when the old parents adopted the child. In the Desus Group, we also believe that the proposal is not enough to meet the criteria for the greatest benefit of children, but it is the first and most important condition that should be met and for which we should always and in any case strive. As regards the removal of parental rights, as proposed in the current proposal for the amendment of the law, the legal profession has made it clear that the general definition of the reasons for the removal of parental rights in the light of the child's benefit is necessary, since it is not possible for the law to provide in advance for all the actions of parents who may have signs of crime and to that extent threaten the child's benefit in order to require the removal of parental rights. Automatism in such a case, as proposed in the draft law, is by no means appropriate, otherwise, in a specific case which would not have been defined in the law, it could have happened that the competent authority could not have responded adequately, as it would not have had a legal basis for doing so. Unfortunately, the proposal for a law is so flawed that the shortcomings could not be corrected by amendments that we do not support in the Desus Group of the bill. The Desus Group has already voted on the working body that the proposal for a new law is not suitable for further consideration. This is how we will vote today. Thank you very much for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u25	The text shall be given by the Group of Unaffiliated Members, on her behalf by Dr. Miriam Bon Klanjšček.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u26	Thank you very much. Your Honor! The proposal of the Law on Marriage and Family Relations, which is before us, interferes with three areas of family law, namely the deprivation of parental rights to parents under the suspicion of criminal service, adoption of grandchildren by old parents and the appointment of a guardian for the child. Initially, I understand the wish of the electorate and the electorate to remedy the shortcomings of the law in force, since the Law on Marriage and Family Relations has not undergone any radical and necessary changes since 1976. However, when we are thoroughly reading the text that we are considering today, it is easy to conclude that the bill offers only partial solutions to legislation, which, to be honest, also contains some controversial elements. Next, I'm going to point out two of the people who disturbed me the most. Even though I strongly condemn any form of violence, especially against children, I cannot go beyond a provision which, on suspicion of a crime, allows the seizure of parental rights by parents, because such a change in legislation would go deeply into the constitutional principle of the presumption of innocence. Taking away parental rights would have punished parents even before his guilt was legally decided. I also need to touch on this occasion on the possibility of adopting grandchildren by the old parents, which the bill envisages. Although this provision eliminates the inadequacies highlighted by the applicant and which have been discussed many times in the past year, the adoption institute is complex in nature among relatives and therefore needs to be assessed in a comprehensive manner in view of the change in relation to the relationship between the two. The bill does not deal with legal consequences that would arise after the adoption of the grandson. This was also pointed out by the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly. In the Alliance and in the Group of Non-Aligned Members, we believe that the change in legislation in the direction proposed by the group of electorates and voters does not offer solutions to ensure adequate corrections to the accumulated problems in the area of family law. A complete overhaul of the area of family law is essential, so on this occasion I welcome the arrival of the Family Code in the procedure of the National Assembly. According to the Ministry, this will improve the situation of children in family relations, ensuring more effective implementation of one basic principle of family law, which is the benefit of children. We hope that's true. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u28	Dear President, dear colleagues and colleagues! Today we are again considering a proposal for a law that puts two fundamental solutions at the forefront. The first gives old parents the opportunity to adopt a grandson. By another solution, parents are given the opportunity to express their will to care for their children in the event of the death of their parents by means of a notary record or in some other unmistakable way. As with the proposed amendment of New Slovenia, this time we also point out the most receptive solution, this is the possibility of adopting a grandson. At the parental board meeting, we once again had the opportunity to listen to the advantages and disadvantages of such an arrangement, but this time we have not supported unfinished solutions. The institution of cohabitation is complex and therefore needs to be regulated in its entirety, and a serious consideration needs to be made primarily on the issue of if and to what extent the relationship between the child and the former relatives changes and the resulting rights and obligations of the child. However, this cannot be adequately regulated by the amendment of only one article, and this time it was not possible to introduce the other necessary provisions with amendments, since not all the articles which would have to be amended, supplemented or re-added by the amendment in question. The adoption of relatives by country is different or acceptable. However, in countries where they are admissible, relatives are listed as possible to a particular knee in a level or by-line; and not only old parents. In most cases, where the adoption of relatives is admissible, there are also other restrictions and specific provisions which the novel under consideration do not contain. Therefore, it would be wise to seek solutions, certainly in the direction of lasting care for children who are not related to adoption, as provided for, for example, by the Family Code within the framework of the Institute of Expanded Parental Rights or Parental Care, which could be given to the most appropriate relative and not only to old parents, taking into account the child’s greatest benefit, of course. Many European countries, however, offer a similar solution, as suggested by the proposer, the opportunity for parents to express their will in advance with regard to the child’s suitable guardian in the event of their death. This institute, of course, is not based on automation, but is judged by the child’s greatest benefit. The parents' will may be challenged in court, as it is necessary to take into account the discretion of the competent authority as to whether the proposed form of child protection is of the greatest benefit to the child. The other two solutions proposed by the proposer concerning the immediate withdrawal of parental rights and the immediate placement of the child under guardianship are already in force, as has been pointed out by the Legislative and Legal Service. Nevertheless, we would like to point out that we want such measures to follow the best interests of the child. However, we believe that with the four partial solutions or the amendment of only four articles of the current law, as proposed in the amendment under consideration, this cannot be ensured. As a result of all of this, we will support the conclusion in the Group of the Parties to the Modern Centre that the law is not suitable for further consideration. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u36	Thank you, Mr. Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! At its regular meeting on 5 January this year, the Government adopted an opinion on the proposal for a law supplementing the Law on Courts, proposed by a group of Members with a first-signed Mr Mag. Tonino. Among the objectives pursued by the proposers in the law, in particular improving the conditions for the judicial treatment of banking offences and enhancing the effectiveness of the courts in the proceedings on these matters, the Government of course supports. It supports the intention of the proposer and supports the objectives of this law, there is no doubt about it. However, according to the Government, this proposed route is not the right solution to achieve this objective for a simple reason – in the Republic of Slovenia, on the basis of the current Law on Courts, we have already established specialised courts operating in four district courts in the Republic of Slovenia. The major crimes committed by these specialised courts and within their jurisdiction are crimes in the field of economic crime. The term banking criminality is a criminological term and covers from a legal point of view, which is relevant to the legal qualification and actual competence of specialised courts, all crimes in the field of economic crime for which a sentence of 5 years or more is prescribed. The Government estimates that this fragmentation, this institutional fragmentation, could also lead to procedural delays or risks, so that such proceedings may take longer because the actual jurisdiction of this court is indirectly determined by the State Prosecutor's Office Act, which provides for the actual competence of the Specialised State Prosecutor's Office, which is established precisely to deal specifically with the detection and prosecution of the most difficult forms of economic crime, including banking, organised and corruption. From an organisational and institutional point of view, it is necessary to take a broader view of the matter, not only from the point of view of one body, which is the most important, crucial in the final stage, namely the courts; but also in this triad from the point of view of the prosecution and the police. Since, at present, 35 judges are involved in specialized courts in the Republic of Slovenia, including banking crime, and in view of the human resources constraints imposed by individual courts, you know that it is one of the important objectives and priorities of the Ministry of Justice and that government to achieve faster procedures through normative, organisational and institutional measures, i.e. faster decision-making. From the point of view of the judiciary, this means that the decision takes place more quickly at the first stage, whatever it already is; from the point of view of the pre-trial procedure, namely detection, police activity and prosecution, and that the available procedural measures are applied and enforced more rapidly, more intensively, more rigorously and more qualitatively according to the criteria of professionalism, which, in its very significant and from this point of view, has given and commanded to all national authorities in this triad also the Constitutional Court of 2014. Since it is essentially this triad, I would like to emphasise that a separate treatment is not sufficient and this approach is not sufficient from an organisational point of view alone. The more important is a comprehensive, more complex approach, and we will ensure what you know, because this has already been made public and public debate is concluded, as well as the cross-cutting coordination of the Criminal Procedure Act by improving the procedural conditions, measures and possibilities for achieving more efficient and faster procedures in the forecasting and penal stages, i.e. during the trial phase. One of the priorities of this government and of the Ministry of Justice is the establishment of a specialised court, because, unlike the proposed solution, which, as it is said, has the same purpose and objective that we are pursuing, we see a solution in the concentration of procedures, so that we are carrying out more rapid individual procedures, as well as the centralisation of institutions, that we have one central court. As we have the National Investigation Office in the predictive phase, as we have the Special Prosecutor's Office in the prosecution phase, we will also have a central court that will specialize only in such matters. This approach seems more effective, more appropriate, even comparatively based on experience. For these reasons, the Government does not support this proposal, regardless of whether it agrees with the intention. Thank you very much.

At the end of the day, this is the third defence proposal for aid for the resolution and restructuring of economic societies and companies in difficulty, the ordinary procedure. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary at the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology, Mrs Eva Stravs Podlogar.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u70	Dear Presidents, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The Ministry of Economic Development and Technology has been granting state aid to economic operators in difficulty under the current Act on Aid for the Rescue and Restructuring of Economic Companies in difficulty since Slovenia's entry into the European Union. The current law has been notified to the European Commission as a state aid scheme and the Ministry is therefore obliged to comply with the European Union guidelines when granting such State aid. Under the general system of state aid in the Republic of Slovenia and the European Union, the process of granting state aid for the resolution and restructuring of economic operators plays a very specific role in difficulties. The volume is also very limited; of the approximately EUR 1 billion of all state aid granted at annual level in the Republic of Slovenia on average in recent years, only EUR 10 to 15 million per year is spent on aid to firms in difficulty on average. The reason is that State aid to firms in difficulty can only justify important social reasons and the need to take into account the strategic role of individual companies in the national economy. On the other hand, it would not be justified to artificially keep a company in the sector with long-term structural overcapacity or if they could only survive through repeated state interventions. Therefore, the use of State aid for rescue and restructuring is only allowed exceptionally, particularly where the extent of the negative economic and social consequences for the country or region and the national cost-benefit analysis justify this. With 1. Seven. In 2014, the European Commission adopted new Community guidelines on State aid for the resolution and restructuring of non-financial corporations in difficulty. Domestic legislation had to be adapted accordingly. The new European Union Guidelines do not bring major systemic changes, but they do bring improvements and modifications that require greater selectivity of such State aids, a clearer definition of eligibility criteria and greater efficiency. More emphasis is placed on the role of market stakeholders in the restructuring process. Taking into account all the required alignment with the new guidelines and the intention for technical and technical improvements to the legal matter, it has been shown that a new proposal for a law has to be drawn up. Let me take a brief look at the key novelties of the new marriage. The definition of the concept of "economic society and cooperatives in difficulty" as set out in Article 8 and is in line with the 2014 guidelines of the European Union; access to rescue and restructuring aid is also being extended to cooperatives in difficulties that have so far been unjustified without an adequate substantive reason being left out of the legal regime; introduction of a new form of aid; temporary support for restructuring; new eligibility criteria for State intervention, which means, in other words, that the intervention is permissible only if the bankruptcy of an economic company or cooperative would cause serious social problems or a large market failure; the newly defined mandatory components of the restructuring programme; mandatory, a state in an economic society or cooperative distribution of the burden of restructuring between the State and the market stakeholders. We are introducing measures relating to conduct which constitute a prohibition on the behaviour of the recipient of State aid with regard to abuses which, on the basis of the aid granted, would prolong the distortion of the market structure or the protection of the beneficiary against healthy competition. In the spirit of simplifying and shortening the procedure, which is crucial in granting State aid to firms, the power to grant State aid is transferred from the Government of the Republic of Slovenia to the Minister for Economic Development and Technology. In conclusion, I would like to point out that, at the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology, we believe that the proposed legal text represents a major step towards the implementation of an effective and Europeanly comparable system of state aid for companies and cooperatives in difficulty. Ladies and gentlemen, I thank you for your attention and at the same time propose that you support the law in the third reading. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u71	Thank you very much. Mrs Iva Dimic will present the position of the New Slovenia Group – Christian Democrats. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u72	Mr President, distinguished representatives of the Ministry, Members and Members of Parliament! The proposal for a law on aid for the rescue and restructuring of companies and cooperatives in difficulty is governed by the intervention or involvement of the State in the functioning of the socio-market economy system. The draft law lays down the conditions for the State to be involved in the resolution of companies and cooperatives. One of these conditions is also the identification of an important systemic role of the company in the development of a country, region or sector that is fulfilled where the bankruptcy and termination of a company or cooperative would result in one or more of the following circumstances: serious social problems with an increased unemployment rate, the emergence of major market distortions and distortions of competition, the risk of disruption of the provision of a significant product or service, the risk of disruption of a significant value chain, the risk of disruption of the operation or destruction of the critical infrastructure of State importance or the loss of significant technical, commercial or professional knowledge and comparative advantages of the State. For small or medium-sized companies, this definition is even more loose. It is enough already to involve a society with high growth potential or significant interconnections with other local and regional companies whose bankruptcy would have negative economic and social consequences for the region or the sector. We can quickly understand that these circumstances are set very loose. The Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly also pointed out that the criteria for assessing a systemicly important role are very general and difficult to verify objectively. Such a definition simply allows for too much potential for abuse. Slovenian taxpayers have in the past been forced to spend too much money to continue playing in this area. The legislative and legal service also pointed out the inconsistencies of the bill's proposal, since it exempts independent entrepreneurs and economic interest associations, which is incomprehensible. In Novi Slovenija, we want a business environment that will allow entrepreneurs to solve their businesses more easily; without having to be solved by the state. Christian Democrats would want to reduce the tax burden on all Slovenian entrepreneurs much more than the cost of saving selected companies. Instead of the new rules provided for in this proposal for assistance, we would like to simplify bureaucratic procedures. According to the OECD, the Republic of Slovenia is one of the countries with the highest share of state ownership in the economy. The bill does not, however, follow a Christian democratic programme direction that the state must withdraw from the economy. In Novi Slovenija we will vote against the proposal for a law on aid for the rescue and restructuring of companies and cooperatives in difficulty.

It is only in this way possible that such companies will also receive eligible State aid. Many times this state aid for restructuring firms is the only way to keep healthy companies on the market or in life. However, we must be aware of this, and it is very strong that we are transferring or giving great powers to the Ministry and the Government to grant State aid autonomously and selectively, of course in exceptional cases. Therefore, procedures should be conducted in a transparent manner, in particular here I would like to draw attention to the auditors, as this should also always require a cost-benefit study by these judges, an analysis of the costs and benefits, which is, in practice, then one basis for determining the eligibility of this State aid in these exceptional cases. It is also very important for the Ministry to establish effective controls for the allocation and further use of these funds. ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u76	Honourable Vice-President of the National Assembly, distinguished representatives of the Ministry and honourable Members and Members of this National Assembly! The Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union lays down, in principle, the prohibition or incompatibility of State aid with the internal market of the European Union. Article 107 of the Treaty provides that any aid from a Member State to individual undertakings which may distort competition by favouring certain undertakings or their products shall not be permitted. The Treaty below provides for exceptions to which State interventions are permitted or compatible with the internal market of the European Union, such as regional and political reasons, the strategic role of the company in the national economy. The Ministry of Economic Development and Technology is carrying out procedures for granting state aid to restructuring companies and cooperatives in difficulty in accordance with the law we are considering today. The law was notified to the European Commission as a state aid scheme on the basis of the 2004 guidelines. The Commission of the European Union shall be of 1. Seven. 2014 adopted new Community guidelines on State aid. The Republic of Slovenia was therefore obliged to supplement the existing law. The new guidelines bring about changes in the direction of greater selectivity, with an emphasis on the effectiveness of the aid granted. The proposed solutions seek to provide greater opportunities for efficient and real restructuring of companies and cooperatives in difficulty and to improve their ability to maintain them, to maintain jobs, and also to improve the functioning of the Slovenian economic system. Aid may be granted in the form of a loan, a subsidy, a guarantee or a capital investment. In doing so, the applicant must provide 25-50 % of his own contribution. The aid is eligible for companies once every 10 years and they must implement measures to limit competition at the time of the restructuring. In practice, State aid has been granted exclusively to companies defined in the law governing companies. The bill also extends the possibility of allocation to cooperatives established under the law governing cooperatives. The cooperatives have so far been unjustifiably released from the legal regime for no adequate substantive reason. In the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we insisted on incorporating guidance from the Smart Specialisation Strategy into the law system. The law will have to take into account the comparative advantages of the domestic economy by limiting the decision-making process to the areas and vision of the Smart Specialisation Strategy, whose activities are focused on sustainable development, ensuring the achievement of climate objectives and developing a sustainable economy. The process of drafting the new law started in spring 2016. At that time, despite the fact that demand or the need to grant State aid to certain companies in difficulty has continued to come from the economic sector, due to the existence of an old State aid scheme on MGRT they have not been able to grant such aid. In this regard, a number of roles have been raised for companies waiting for priority treatment. In the discussion before the vote on the proposed decision, some members of the Committee supported price deregulation and considered it excessively feared that oil suppliers would raise the price. The view was also expressed that the government should act towards full deregulation of prices, but rather take measures in the field of excise policy. Some discussers pointed out that the proposal does not support the law because the transposition of such measures into the legal framework is not the right way. On the contrary, some members of the Committee and the proposers felt that, in the event of deregulation, margins and prices of oil derivatives would rise. Following the discussion, the Committee adopted, pursuant to Article 128(2) of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, that the proposal for a law on the pricing of petroleum derivatives was not suitable for further consideration. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u89	Thank you very much. I give the floor to the representative of the Government to present my position on the proposal of the parent working body. State Secretary Eva Stravs Podlogar, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u90	Honourable Presidents, honourable Members and Members. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia received an opinion on the proposal for a law on the pricing of petroleum derivatives submitted to the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia on 16 November 2016 for consideration by the Group of Members and Members. According to the Group of Members and Members, the purpose of the proposal for a law is to introduce price regulation for 95-octane unleaded motor gasoline, 98- or multi-octane unleaded motor gasoline used in road or maritime transport, diesel fuel and extra light combustion oil. With regard to the solutions proposed, I would like to explain the following. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia advocates a competitive economy and the free operation of businesses on the market. The proposal for a law is largely similar to the former regulation on the pricing of certain oil derivatives. The draft law thus provides for the reregulation of the prices of all oil derivatives, contrary to the recently adopted decision of the Government on the gradual deregulation or liberalisation of the prices of petroleum derivatives. The measures provided for in the Price Control Act, which provides for the adoption of successive regulations on the pricing of petroleum derivatives, are exceptional in nature and should last for a maximum period of 6-12 months, followed by a gradual abolition of those measures. In Slovenia, however, gas prices have been regulated under this law since April 2000, i.e. for more than 15 years. Currently, only the prices of the only textbooks per class are regulated in Slovenia in addition to the prices of certain oil derivatives. In recent years other energy markets, such as electricity, natural gas, and district heating, have also been deregulated or systematically regulated. Feared that oil distributors would significantly increase margins after price liberalisation and thus increase retail prices of petroleum derivatives, there are several reasons why. Let us explain that the margin in the overall structure of the retail price of petroleum derivatives represents only 6 %, which means that the final retail price is largely dependent on other price variables, in particular on the price of oil on global markets and the price of excise duty, which represents around 40% in the retail price structure. The world market price for oil, together with taxes, represents around 90% of the retail price and therefore the possible margin increase would have a relatively small impact on the final price of individual oil derivatives. In other words, the final retail price of petroleum derivatives depends to a large extent on other variables. The price of oil in Slovenia, as elsewhere in the European Union, depends mainly on the price of oil on the world market and on the amount of the other charges that make up the final price. Before any further decisions, i.e. the reregulation of oil derivatives prices or further liberalisation, we will carry out an analysis of the consequences of price release in 2016. While the Government of the Republic of Slovenia may reintroduce the regulation of the prices of petroleum derivatives if the actual impact of the free operation of the prices of petroleum derivatives on the market would result in a greater negative impact on public finances, the economy and the household, as previously foreseen. The aim of the State is to establish a normal functioning market where market mechanisms and competition between oil derivatives distributors can be established. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia therefore opposes the solutions contained in the proposal for a law on the pricing of petroleum derivatives submitted by a group of Members and Members and considers that the law is not suitable for further consideration. Thank you very much.

In the meantime, when we expect the results of the analysis, the Joint Left Group has introduced a law on the pricing of oil derivatives into the parliamentary procedure. The objective of the proposal is to re-establish price regulation. They also advocate the transfer of competence to the National Assembly to decide on this issue from the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. With regard to the transfer of competences, I quote the opinion of the Legal and Legislative Service: “The possibility of a public intervention in the free pricing is systematically regulated by the Price Control Act, which leaves the use of illegal price-setting measures to the Government. The proposed legislation thus re-establishes an already regulated relationship. Since this does not at the same time govern the relationship between the two laws, which are adapted in terms of the hierarchy of legal acts, there may be a conflict between the two laws, which, as such, violates the principle of the rule of law of Article 2 of the Constitution.” We respect the opinion of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. However, the final decision towards deregulation will only be drawn up after a thorough analysis of the oil derivatives price analysis prepared by the Ministry of Economy. We have always advocated an open market, without unnecessary administrative obstacles and regulations in the Group of the Modern Centre Party, which will make it easier for the economy to breathe. At the same time, it advocates consumer interest so that it is possible to reintroduce regulation if the actual impact of free pricing of oil derivatives on the market would have a greater negative impact on public finances, the economy and the household. The proposal of the Joint Left Group, which is only a regulation and does not take into account the comprehensive analysis of the market in petroleum derivatives, which requires the observation of a certain period of price movement, will not be supported. Thank you very much.

We therefore consider and give guidance to the government. People get distracted, Austrian buyers don't come to Slovenia. We are, as the speaker said before me, a transit country; our transit positions and an optimized model of price calculation are not yet available, and this is a specific feature of the Republic of Slovenia. And it's less. This means less excise duty, more margin room for manoeuvre, as a result our petrol services can adapt to Austria, neighbouring Croatia or Italy; daily. And this must be a model that will have to be in force in the future in the Republic of Slovenia. If you do not have “Spielraum”, as the Germans would say, a manoeuvre space, then our suppliers of oil derivatives at this time cannot adapt because the margin is so low that they cannot compete with Austrian suppliers across the border. The current biggest consequence is that there is no such pressure in Ljubljana; petrol services at the borders, especially in the environment where I come from, our part of Styria, Radgona, Lenart and others, citizens are already going to Austria. It is similar on the Slovenian Koroška side, where they go to Austria's Koroška. Our model is not good, deregulation, just the first step, we are talking about complete deregulation, and the next step must also establish a competition guardian who must set some criteria so that other oil derivatives providers can come and operate marketably in our business environment. Therefore, as I said at the outset, the proposal for a law on the pricing of oil derivatives prepared by the United Left cannot be supported in any way in the Slovenian Democratic Party. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u100	Thank you, Vice President. The fact that deregulation of the prices of oil derivatives for the country and citizens is a detrimental measure is a single profession, consumer organisations and also those government services that have prepared studies on the consequences of such a policy on public finances. The only ones who disagree with this are oil lobbying on the one hand and their mercenaries on the other. Of course, that makes sense. They are the only ones who will benefit from price deregulation. The government has clearly stepped on their side and the falanga of neoliberal apologists under the leadership of Minister Restovalska is determined to increase their profits at higher fuel prices, which we will all pay. For professional arguments, even their own, they are not taken up and with religious zeal they repeat floscules about the salvation of the free market. This forced us in the United Left to file the law that we are considering today and which is intended to reintroduce price regulation for all oil derivatives. The chronic argument for price deregulation is that this will bring competition to the oil derivatives market. As a result, the long-term margins of traders should not be raised or, in their view, at the current level. The government therefore acknowledges, on the one hand, that prices will undoubtedly rise; on the other hand, that the measure, if not already harmful, is at least pointless because margins will remain at the same level. In addition, we are convinced that the fact that, after deregulation of the prices on the motorway cross, the margins have not been raised is evidence that this will also be the case in the event of a complete deregulation. However, it is precisely the fact that the margins of all suppliers at the motorway cross remain exactly the same as the Regulation on certain maximum margins on other sales services, proof that we have a duopol which contractually maintains prices at the same level and that the market has no impact on the margin. Traders are smart, waiting for a state of complete deregulation. If we raised margins along the highway, we could, for a short period, increase the profit from the sale of fuel on these petrol services; and in the medium term, we would lose much more profits from sales on all petrol services. The best example of what lies ahead for us in the event of complete deregulation is the movement of a fully deregulated price of fuel oil. Last spring and most of the summer were still comparable to other members. Therefore, traders took advantage of the first half of September and the second half of October when, in order to encourage other fuels, the buyers assumed that the fuel oil would also be encouraged. Then, in the first half of September, the prices of heating oil decreased by slightly less than 3% throughout the European Union, for example, by 2.5% in Austria; in Slovenia, prices increased by 10% during this period. However, since the price of gasoline rose simultaneously, customers of the fuel oil could expect higher prices of the fuel oil; thus, the drastic increase in the margin remained undetected. The government and the right are convincing us that deregulation of oil derivatives prices is necessary because fuel prices are much higher in our country than in neighbouring countries where prices are already deregulated. This is true, but this is due to high fuel taxes, which remain unchanged with price deregulation. Only the maximum allowed margin is lifted, which traders can account for themselves, which operates in the oligopolistic market. This margin is the lowest in Europe, but it is considered limited only upstream. Any cheaper, if you want to compete more, the supplier could already sell fuel cheaper, but because of oligopolo, only few choose to do so. We are moving this maximum allowed margin through our proposal for a law from a sub-legislative to a legislative level. The purpose of this law is to prevent harmful and to underpin the Government's ideological decision to deregulation of oil derivatives prices. We will therefore vote against the conclusion that the law is not suitable for further consideration.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u102	Mr President, representatives of the Ministry, Members and Members of Parliament! In the Republic of Slovenia, the system of socio-market economy is supposed to apply. Nevertheless, our legal arrangements are still far from market economy principles. One of these areas is the regulation of the prices of petroleum derivatives. Thank God this is one of the areas where price regulation withdraws in principle from the free market. In recent times, the government has gradually phased out the pricing system for oil derivatives through several measures, as we welcome Christian Democrats. That is why in Novi Slovenija we oppose the measures contained in the proposal for a law on pricing of petroleum derivatives, which restores the situation in this area at a time of limiting free pricing. The proposal introduces a margin limit for 95-octane unleaded motor gasoline, 98- or multi-octane unleaded motor gasoline, diesel and extra light combustion oil. In view of the socialist political orientation of the United Left, which is the drafter in this bill, we understand their aspirations for re-regulation of prices on the market. Such a proposal may appeal to a lot of people at first sight, but such a sharp restriction is contrary to the basic principles of the functioning of the market economy on which successful global economies are based. Restricting the prices of petroleum derivatives also constitutes an intervention in the constitutional right to free economic initiative. Any such intervention must be justified by strong arguments that would be useful to him. The argument for a measure that would so severely affect the socio-market economy system, we do not see the proposal for a law in New Slovenia. In a study prepared by Dr. Igor Masten and Dr. Aljos Feldin, the authors found that the potential for price increases due to deregulation was limited. The reasons for the limited margin increase in the event of price liberalisation are, after their study at a high level of taxation in relation to border markets, the high response to demand for derivatives in Slovenia, the price of smallness and the express transit nature of the Slovenian market and the possible entry of new providers. In Slovenia, we must separate ourselves from the idea that there is always and everywhere a state intervention, that almost endlessly it is necessary to regulate and interfere with any social or economic activity that takes place in our country. For interfering with the market system and the constitutional right to free economic initiative, which such regulation of the prices of petroleum derivatives brings, we do not see any valid reasons in Novi Slovenija. Therefore, we will not support the proposal for a law on pricing of oil derivatives.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u120	The draft law was tabled by the Government. In order to further explain the draft law, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, Minister of the Interior Mag. Spring Györkös Žnidar.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u121	President, thank you for your word, honourable Members, honourable Members! On the table we have amendments to the Foreigners Act, which is extremely demanding in its mother, because it is necessary to understand the broader context of the problem and the wide range of social consequences that may occur in the case of the passivity of the Republic of Slovenia. The law was therefore subject to a very comprehensive and very intensive coordination process in government, coalition and also in the National Security Council, which it supported. We also worked intensively with the Ministry of Justice, and the Green Light was also given to us by the Government's Law Service. In the event of a changed migration situation, the Republic of Slovenia may face a situation which may pose a threat to public order and internal security. The basic purpose of the law is to prevent such a situation so that Slovenia can also go towards effective management of mass illegal immigration in a changed situation. The fundamental point is that migration is also a security issue. The second thing I would like to stress is that the law is written as a last resort to an emergency situation where Slovenia is no longer able to implement the International Protection Act due to overload. And thirdly, the law provides the legal basis for timely action so that no emergency can occur. We have already heard several times that Slovenia can limit the right to asylum only when emergency situations arise; I say that waiting for an emergency as part of a solution to the Government or the Ministry of the Interior is not acceptable. Moreover, such passive waiting for emergencies is unacceptable and even constitutionally controversial. The responsible authorities must do everything to ensure that no emergency occurs. Slovenia is a responsible and credible member of the European Union, participates in the search for European solutions and is active in the implementation of European solidarity. The European Union is a single area of freedom, security and rights, based on the concept of the protection of external borders and also on the concept of access to international protection in the European Union as a whole. This is accessibility to the Geneva Convention procedure. The essence of this Convention is to prohibit the return of persecuted persons to a country in which their freedom and security would be threatened; this is not applicable in any way if they return to the safe Member States of the European Union. The principle of non-refoulement does not guarantee the automatic right of entry and residence in the country. The persons to whom entry may be refused come from a secure Member State of the European Union and therefore the non-refoulement under the Geneva Convention is not violated. All Member States of the European Union, as we know, are considered safe under the Amsterdam Treaty Protocol, followed by the rest of the European Union legislation, particularly the Dublin Regulation, which is also based on the presumption and also allows automatic surrender of applicants between Member States. Of course, Dublin can only be abandoned in the event of objectively identified systemic deficiencies in the asylum procedure and the reception condition. The aim of the European asylum system must be to protect those who really need protection, while preventing abuse. Economic migration cannot be resolved within the asylum system. The asylum system cannot serve as a migration channel, as it can easily break the asylum system. Therefore, there is no institution and convention that could require Slovenia to wait so long for an emergency situation or a severe threat to public order and internal security. After all, human rights are limited to the rights of others, so the country must weigh, in a state of overburden, between the rights of foreigners and the rights of its own nationals or residents of the Republic of Slovenia. Therefore, the text of the law takes into account the proportionality shown by the fact that Slovenia can face the burdens, but has a legal basis for action when the point of the break is reached, which will be decided by the National Assembly as the highest sovereign in that country, i.e. by the representatives of the people. If the European Union does not find a solution that is sustainable and effective, Slovenia has not only a legitimate right but also a duty to cut down on those national measures that are necessary to protect its interests. Slovenia's capabilities are objectively limited. The fact that migration is also a security issue is not an invention of the Ministry of the Interior, so is the Resolution on the National Security Strategy; security must be protected in any case. I therefore once again call on Members and Members to support today's amendments to the Foreigners Act. Thank you.

In an additional explanation for the articles, the representative of the Minister of the Interior said that the fundamental reason for the amendment of the Foreign Affairs Act was that Slovenia could face a situation which could pose a threat to public order and internal security in the event of a changed migration situation. The responses of other countries in the neighbourhood to possible new waves of migration are expected to be different from those of the past, which could lead to a massive increase in requests for international protection. This can bring with it the potential of abuse of an asylum system that can lead to its breakdown. As a result, this fracture can have multiple effects, which can manifest itself as a serious threat to public order and internal security at an extreme stage. Slovenia, as the guardian of the Schengen border, has an extremely high responsibility to prevent the deterioration of security throughout the Schengen area, which means that it must take all the measures necessary to maintain control over illegal migration. We are in a difficult position because the borders are closing north of us, so Slovenia is the first country before closed doors in case there is a re-mass wave of migration. Slovenia has neither the capacity nor the conditions for dealing with mass migration flows. The final European solution that Slovenia is helping to create has not yet come to light, which means that in the event of the cancellation of migration pressure mechanisms, in particular at the level of the European Union, it has the right or duty to take all national measures to ensure that we do not face an uncontrollable situation in the extreme phase. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service summed up the substance of the conclusion of the written opinion of the ZPS. In doing so, she stressed that this is an extremely sensitive content which also affects human rights and fundamental freedoms. Such an intervention always requires a thorough constitutional and international debate, respecting the requirement for the constitutionality and legality of the proposed solutions, in particular the principle of clarity and certainty. In her presentation, the Director of the NGO Law and Information Centre stressed that Slovenia still has an extremely low share of applicants for international protection in relation to the European average; nor did the asylum system break when applications were increased. It noted that the drafting of the bill did not follow what was stated in the Resolution on Regulatory Activities. The representative of Amnesty International Slovenia considered that the draft law provided for measures which were in striking contradiction with the basic principles of human rights, namely the right to an effective asylum procedure, the prohibition of collective expulsions, the right to an effective remedy and the principle of non-refoulement. The Director of the Peace Institute has pointed out that Slovenia is a party to the Geneva Convention regardless of its domestic legislation; and it is under this Convention to accept and treat applications for asylum for all foreigners. However, the bill provides that the police will not accept requests at all unless the person is tried in one of the four explicitly listed categories, which is contrary to the Geneva Convention. In the debate, the coalition MPs welcomed the solutions to the bill because it was an important area of social life, which was under severe test in 2015. Inadequately, they may even find it dangerous that Slovenia should wait for a state of emergency, as defined and laid down in Articles 92 and 16 of the Constitution. ParlaMint-SI_2017-01-26-SDZ7-Redna-26.ana.u127	Thank you very much again, President. The migration situation has changed significantly over the last six months. We expect and we want the European Union to come up with uniform procedures regarding the challenges posed by migration. The Republic of Slovenia has always supported and actively cooperated in the search for common solutions at European Union level, but until there are legal solutions, we as a country have a sovereign right and a duty to take all measures that enable public order, security and the smooth functioning of the country, including in the case of mass migration, disproportionate pressure on the asylum system. The proposal for a law is based on the assumption that it is not a matter of repealing human rights and establishing an emergency, but rather on restricting human rights to asylum within the limits of Articles 15, 32 and 48 of the Constitution, since the Republic of Slovenia will still accept certain vulnerable groups while at the same time guaranteeing all rights to those who already have or have been asked for international protection in the Republic of Slovenia. This is a measure that would have been introduced if public order and internal security were threatened, which could have made it difficult for the central institutions, as already mentioned, to operate. The latter is set out in the amendment to the new Article 5a, which was adopted at the meeting of the parent working body. The amendment provides that Article 10b shall not enter into force or apply as from the date of the entry into force of this Act; only when the situation in the field of migration has changed.
